The prosecutor did not appear NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-227]
1240. JAMES McPHERSON was indicted for embezzlement.
RICHARD TWYNEHAM. I am a newspaper agent; the prisoner was in my employ on the 10th of April - he received money for me, which he was to pay immediately he came home. On the 10th of April he paid me no money, but said he had lost 7s. out of his pocket, which he had received from Cliff - I gave him in charge.
SAMUEL PIGGOTT CLIFF. On the 10th of April I paid the prisoner 7s. and a farthing on account of Mr. Twyneham; I am sure there was one half-crown and one shilling among it - I generally pay every Saturday; the prisoner brought the bill on Saturday; I offered him a sovereign; he had no change I gave him what silver I had - on Monday he brought the bill again, and the balance was not down - he said he would call on Tuesday morning, which he did about ten o'clock, and I paid him the 7s.
JOHN LACEY. I took him in charge. He said he had lost the money; I said, "Where from?" and he showed me a hole in his pocket - he said he had got no money, but I found a sixpence and some copper on him.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-228]
1241. JEREMIAH MAHONEY was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of May, 1 boot, value 4s., the goods of Dennis Sullivan.
DENNIS SULLIVAN. I am a bootmaker, and live on Saffron-hill. On the 28th of May, about eight o'clock in the evening, I missed a boot; and seeing two boys near the door (one of whom was a bad character). I went out and pursued - a man brought them back, but neither of them had it - soon after another man, whom I had sent out, brought in the prisoner with the boot.
JAMES HALL. I was sent for an officer, and saw the two boys run away - the prisoner stood at the corner of a court with the boot buttoned in his jacket; I took him back to the shop - I had not seen him near.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-229]
1242. JAMES FITZPATRICK and HANNAH, HIS WIFE, were indicted for stealing, on the 13th of May, 13 plates, value 1s. 1d.; 3 dishes, value 6d.; 1 salt-cellar, value 1d., and 3 spoons, value 6d., the goods of Daniel Larter.
DANIEL LARTER. I keep the Gun public-house, Shoreditch . On the 13th of May, about two o'clock, I was in the cellar; an alarm was given; I came up, and missed these things from the kitchen; I went to the prisoner's room, which is about thirty yards off, about eight o'clock at night, and found them all - the man said he did not know they were there; he had handed me a candle to look for them, but I found them out on the stairs - the woman said she had brought them there.
Prisoner J. FITZPATRICK. Q. Did I not say to her, "What business had you to bring them here?" A. Yes; the woman had been drinking, but knew what she was doing. I had seen them both at my house that afternoon; they were customers.
J. FITZPATRICK's Defence. I had some friends from Ireland, and got intoxicated - I did not know what I did.
HANNAH FITZPATRICK - GUILTY. Aged 26.
Strongly recommended to Mercy, being pregnant.
Confined Nine Days.
JAMES FITZPATRICK - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-230]
1243. JOHN DOUBLEDAY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 1 shirt, value 1s.; 1 shift, value 6d.; 2 pair of stockings, value 2d., and 3 handkerchiefs, value 6d., the goods of Thomas Longes, and 1 shirt, value 5s., the goods of John Black.
HANNAH LONGES. I am the wife of Thomas Longes, we live at Islington. On the 13th of April I hung these articles in the back garden - I saw them safe at six o'clock, and missed them at eight - I found them in Key's possession next morning.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. Between eight and nine o'clock on the night of the 13th, the prisoner came up to me with the property in a bundle, and inquired his road - I suspected something wrong, and followed him down Vittoria-street; he went to jump over a ditch - I ran and jumped over also, we both fell into the ditch - I secured him.
FRANCES BLACK. I lodge with Longes; this is my husband's night-shirt; his name is John.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A person threw away a bundle in the street, he was running, but there was no cry of Stop thief! it being dark I fell into the ditch; somebody came up with this bundle.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-231]
1244. THOMAS CLARK and JOHN WILLIAMS were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of April, 1 table, value 16s.; 1 bed-cover, value 1s. 6d.; 2 blankets, value 2s.; 1 coat, value 5s.; 1 waistcoat, value 2s.; 1 shirt, value 2s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s.; 1 comforter, value 9d.; 1 pair of stockings, value 1s., and 1 jacket, value 6d., the goods of John Lewin.
JOHN LEWIN. I live in Charles-street, Bromley, and am a chimney-sweeper. On the 24th of April, between three and four o'clock in the morning, I lost this property, which was safe just before - I had taken Clark to my house the day before, to assist me to move.
JAMES DARBY. I am a sweep. On the 24th of April I and Fetch were helping to move Lewin's things in a cart - I had sent Clark there the day before; while we were loading, I missed the table.
RICHARD FETCH. I am a carman. I was moving the goods - Clark was there, and while we were moving the things, the table was taken.
JAMES FORREST. I live at Bromley. On the 24th of April, I was getting up between eight and nine o'clock, Williams came into my room with a bundle, and asked me to let him leave it, saying that a poor man with ten children had been seized on for rent; he left the bundle, and came up again, and asked me to let him leave this table - I gave him leave - one Kennedy who is now dead, was with him.
JANE FORREST. On the 25th of April the officers came to my house, and took these things from my son's room, of which I gave them the key.
JOHN TWEEDY. On the 24th of April, about eight o'clock in the morning, Lewin and Darby came and gave me information - I went and found Clark in the privy of a house; the door was fastened - I said, "You may as well come out," which he did - I took him to the watch-house - Lewin came and tapped him on the shoulder, andsaid, "My lad, you have got my jacket on" - he said,"You gave me that" - Lewin replied, "We will see about that by and by" - I received information, and found the goods at Forrest's.
JAMES FORDHAM. I was with Tweedy, and found the property at Forrest's.(Property produced and sworn to.)
CLARK's Defence. I understood Lewin expected to be seized on, and took the things to Forrest's.
JOHN LEWIN. I never gave him the jacket - I said perhaps I might give him one when he had done.
WILLIAMS' Defence. Clark asked me to let him leave the things with me - my room being small I asked Forrest to mind them. CLARK - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
WILLIAMS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-232]
1245. THOMAS CLAYTON was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of May, 18 shillings, the monies of William Wilkins Wilton.
WILLIAM WILKINS WILTON. I am a bookseller, and live in Holborn. The prisoner was about three weeks in my employ - I gave him 18 shillings to take to St. John's-square, to buy some books at Mr. Dove's.
WILLIAM HEWITT. I am a painter, and live in St. Martin's lane. I was in Wilton's shop on the 16th of May, and heard of the prisoner being sent out - I was going to my lodging the same evening, and saw him in a cook-shop; I gave him in charge; he said he had spent nearly all the money, with three more boys.
WILLIAM LEMON. I am a constable, and took the prisoner - I found only a half-crown on him.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270531-233]
1246. JAMES BOLTON, JAMES STILES, and JOHN ASSITER were indicted for stealing, on the 28th of April, 1 pair of plough-chains, value 4s.; 1 chain-bow and pin, value 6d., and 1 plough-hammer, value 6d., the goods of John Draper.
ROBERT COX. I work for Mr. John Draper, a farmer, who lives at Hillingdon. I had been ploughing with this plough, at two o'clock on Friday, the 27th of April, and left it with these other things in the field; the next morning I came back to work, and missed them.
EDWARD SWEENEY. I am a constable. On Saturday, the 28th of April, I saw a horse and cart with these artiticles in it - I took possession of them - the prisoners were afterwards brought by another witness.
WILLIAM COLLETT. I am a butcher, and live at Harefield. On the 28th of April, at half-past seven o'clock in the evening, the three prisoners were at Harefield, about six miles from Hillingdon, I followed them, and saw in the cart, which they had with them, two old iron hoops - when I got to Ryeslip I saw these things all taken from the cart, and gave the prisoners into the constable's hands - they said they had bought them at Drayton, which is two miles and a half from Uxbridge - when they saw me in pursuit of them they gallopped as fast as they could.(Property produced and sworn to.)
STILES' Defence. We bought these plough-chains for 6d. of a poor man.
ASSITER'S Defence. I was going about Drayton, calling for iron and bones - a man called and asked me if I would have these chains.
WILLIAM BOLTON. I am uncle of the prisoner Bolton. Assiter and him worked for me - I sent them out every day to collect these things - I keep a marine-store shop - Stiles never belonged to me, but came to them in a morning to go with them.
BOLTON - GUILTY. Aged 14.
STILES - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Confined Six Weeks.
ASSITER - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Six Weeks and Whipped.
There was another indictment against Bolton and Stiles, which was not tried.
[trialAccount t18270531-234]
1247. HORATIO JAMES ELLIOTT was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of April, 17lbs. weight of lead, value 3s., belonging to Isaac Wilkinson, and fixed to his dwelling-house.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to be fixed to a building.
ISAAC WILKINSON. I have a house, No. 33, Duck-lane, Westminster. On the 1st of May, in consequence of information, I went there, and found the whole of the gutter was gone - some of it appeared fresh cut away - I had seen it all safe three weeks before.
THOMAS PARKER. On the 1st of May, Mr. Wilkinson informed me of this lead being taken - I went to Queen-square, and saw Phillips, who produced some lead, which I compared with the gutter - it matched exactly with the small pieces which are left - it appeared fresh cut.
DAVID PHILLIPS. I am an officer. On the 26th of April I found the prisoner going along Duck-lane with this lead - I asked what he had got; he said, "Some tools;" I felt it - he then said, "You know what I have got, take me;" I took him - he told me he found it in Horseferry-road, at the watch-house - he was remanded, and I found the prosecutor - I saw it compared with his house - it fitted exactly.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I was constable of the night - I searched, and found this large knife in his pocket - the edges were blue, as if it had been cutting lead, and his hands also appeared blue.
Prisoner's Defence. I found the lead and knife by a shoemaker's window.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-235]
1248. ANN YELLOP was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of May, 2 sovereigns, 1 half-crown, 2 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the monies of William Mears, from his person.
WILLIAM MEARS. On Saturday, the 12th of May, about half-past eleven o'clock at night, I met the prisoner in Berkeley-street - she wished me to go with her; I refused to have any thing to do with her, but she walked by my side; I felt her hand in my pocket; she pulled her hand out, and put it to her mouth - I felt, and missed two sovereigns and five shillings; I took from her mouth a sovereign, a half-crown, and two shillings, before the watchman came up - the rest has not been found.
JOHN NICHOLSON. I am a watchman. I was in Dover-yard, and heard the prisoner making a noise - I went up, and the prosecutor said she had robbed him, and he had just taken this money from her mouth; he said hewanted another sovereign and a sixpence, but they have not been found.
Prisoner's Defence. He wanted to go with me, but he was too tipsy - he gave me a few shillings - he called me back, and said, "Give me that money back;" I would not, and put it into my mouth; he called the watchman, and gave me in charge.
JOHN NICHOLSON. He seemed to have drank a little, but knew what he was about.
WILLIAM MEARS. I did not give her the money.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-236]
1249. EDWARD WALKER and JOSEPH EDWARDS were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 1 half-crown, 1 pair of shoes, value 3s.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d., and 2 knives, value 1s., the property of David Profit, from his person.
RICHARD KENDRICK. I am a watchman. On the morning of the 8th of April, at half-past one o'clock, I was in Wellclose-square, and turning into Parson's-street, I saw David Profit on the ground, very drunk - Walker was taking off his shoes; Edwards had his hand in his right-hand pocket - I sprung my rattle, ran and overtook Edwards turning into Well-street, but found nothing on him - I lost sight of Walker for some time, but am certain of him.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. What distance were you off? A. Seven or eight yards; Walker was at his feet, and he put something under his arm, I cannot say what; I did not see the shoes; Profit had himself been taken to the watch-house on that night before.
ISAAC PORRITT. The prisoners were brought to the watch-house, and searched, one half crown was found on Edwards, but nothing which the prosecutor could swear to; he is now at sea - he had no shoes on.
MATTHEW HALL. I heard an alarm, and took Walker, who was running.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-237]
1250. JOHN BARNETT was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of April, 2 sovereigns, the monies of Nathaniel Skuse, from his person.
WILLIAM HEATH. I am a watchman of Whitechapel. On the 23d of April I was in Osborne-street, at half-past 12 o'clock at night. I know Nathaniel Skuse, he lives in Wentworth-street; he was in Osborne-street, very drunk - and the prisoner leading him. I followed and saw the prisoner take his right-hand from Skuse's right-hand pocket. I went up and asked if he was his friend - he said he was - I said, "I think you have robbed him." I took him to the watch-house - Skuse, said in his presence, that he had lost two sovereigns. The officer found two sovereigns in the prisoner's fob pocket.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did you not see a friend of Skuse's there? A. Yes; the prisoner did not say he had asked him to assist; he is not here, nor is Skuse.
THOMAS HAILES. I was officer of the night. Skuse was brought in, and said he had been robbed of two sovereigns by the prisoner, who had laid hold of his arm, and put his hand into his pocket - the prisoner said he had only got 7s. I searched him and found that. I then said"I'll search your fob," which I did, and found two sovereigns. I then searched the prosecutor and found some silver on him, his right-hand breeches pocket was turned inside out - he had a bundle of papers.
Cross-examined. Q. Where was the silver? A. In his waistcoat-pocket; his friend was not bound over.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming home and saw this man laying insensible on the ground - a man, calling himself his friend, asked me to assist; the watchman came up, and asked if I was his friend - I said "No; but I was assisting;" he said he thought he saw my hand come from his pocket. I can prove how I got the 2l. 7s.; the prosecutor afterwards said he had lost nothing. I declare my innocence - the prosecutor was locked up himself, as he was drunk - his friend has not been heard of since.
WM. HEATH. The prosecutor was locked up - he did say afterwards, that he had lost nothing; his friend was close by him.
WILLIAM YOUNG. I am a carman and live in Mercer's-row, Shadwell. On the 23d of April I paid the prisoner 2l. 10s. for a suit of clothes. I gave him two sovereigns and a half in gold - this was about seven o'clock in the evening. GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-238]
1251. ISAAC JACOBS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of May, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of John Wood, from the person of Jane his wife.
JANE WOOD. I am the wife of John Wood. I was in Church-row, Whitechapel on the 1st of May, about half-past nine o'clock at night, the prisoner came and took this handkerchief out of my basket. I was running at the time, and collared him immediately, he begged of me to let him go.
Cross-examined by Mr. BARRY. Q. Where was this? A. Near a sugar-warehouse - there were some ballan-singers there - I was running past, my handkerchief was picked up - I lost two sixpences from my basket.
ROBERT COMBER. I heard a cry of Stop thief! the prisoner was running - he had got from the prosecutrix. I collared him and took him to the watch-house.
THOMAS POWELL. I saw a gentleman pick up the handkerchief. I shewed it to the prosecutrix and took it to the watch-house. I returned and saw something shining on the ground. and found two sixpences - that was between nine and ten o'clock at night.
THOMAS HAILES. I heard a cry of Stop thief! and found the prisoner in Comber's hands.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I heard an alarm and was stopped.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-239]
1252. GEORGE McANDREWS and JOSEPH WILKINSON were indicted for stealing, on the 17th of May, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of Malcolm Currie, from his person.
MALCOLM CURRIE. On the 17th of May, about ten o'clock at night, I was opposite to Messrs. Coutts, in the Strand. A person asked if I had lost my handkerchief. I felt and it was gone - he said they had run up a court. I gave him my address, and next morning he brought my handkerchief to me. I found the prisoner at the office.(Property produced and sworn to.)
ANGELIOUS BERTRAUN. On the 17th of May I was in the Strand, and saw the two prisoners going after several gentlemen; they then met the prosecutor nearly opposite Round-court, and I saw Wilkinson take the handkerchief from his pocket. I told Roberts (who was with me), to tell the gentleman; I watched them up a court. I saw them looking at the handkerchief under a gas-light, and McAndrew then put it into his hat.
THOMAS ROBERTS. I saw the prisoners behind Mr. Currie. I went to him - he gave me his address; we went down Round-court, and took the prisoners.
McANDREWS' Defence. I met a young man who asked me to buy a handkerchief for 2s. 6d.
WILKINSON'S Defence. I went up the court, and was taken - but am entirely innocent.
McANDREWS - GUILTY. Aged 17.
WILKINSON - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-240]
1253. JAMES EASTMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of May, 2 books, value 7s., the goods of George Collier, his master.
GEORGE COLLIER. The prisoner was in my employ. I am a bookbinder to the British and Foreign Bible Society, in Earl-street. These two Bibles were mine, as being in their employ - these are them.
LEONARD MATHEWS. I keep a sale-shop. On the 17th of May a man called and asked if I would buy some Bibles. I said Yes; and on the 18th the prisoner brought two, for which he asked 3s., and asked if I could take seven a week of him - this created some suspicion. I told him to leave them, and come back - I kept the Bibles; he did not return.
JOHN VANN. I received these Bibles from Mathews. I went to the Bible Society's house, and found the prisoner there.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-241]
1254. ELIZABETH WRIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of April, 1 shilling, the monies of Peter Howard; 2 sheets, value 4s.; 1 pillow, value 1s.; 1 bolster, value 1s. 6d., and 4 curtains, value 4s., the goods of Peter Howard, in a lodging-room.
ANN HOWARD. I am the wife of Peter Howard - we live in Hart-street, Hoxton; he is insane. On the 11th of April I let the prisoner a furnished room - I found she was pawning things; I went to her room with an officer, and asked what she had done with these things - she said she had sent the curtains to be washed. I missed the other things.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN BURGESS. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Old-street. These sheets and curtains were pawned by the prisoner, on the 12th and 13th of April.
RENTON NICHOLSON. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned a pillow and bolster with me.
JOSEPH BOYCE. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner, and found the duplicate on her.
Prisoner's Defence. I was much distressed, but expected to receive money, and redeem them.
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.

Third Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1255. DANIEL GILES was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of May, 1 half-sovereign, 2 half-crowns, 3 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the monies of Frederick Hatch.
The prisoner pleaded GUILTY.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-243]
1256. JOHN ASHBY was indicted for embezzlement.
SECOND COUNT, for stealing two half-crowns, the monies of William Henry Gardiner.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM HENRY GARDINER. I am an ironmonger, and live in Norton-falgate. The prisoner was my shopman - he was to put what money he received into the till. In consequence of suspicion I marked 28s. in half-crowns and shillings - it was marked on the tail; there was no more in the till. I went to Mr. Hems, and gave him four half-crowns and two shillings, marked money, to send a person to my shop, to lay out, on the morning of the 1st of May - there was then 16s. in the till. I went out, leaving the prisoner in care of the shop - I examined the till about ten minutes past nine o'clock, and found 34s. in it; I had seen other customers at the shop; there was but 23s. of the marked money. I went to Hems - he returned with me; I called the prisoner into the counting-house, and told him I missed money out of the till; he appeared confused, and denied having taken any - I called Hems in, who said to him, "I know all about it - come pull out what money you have in your pocket;" he then pulled out two of the half-crowns which I had marked; Hems said, "I can swear to these;" he had other money, not marked in his pocket.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Had you put any marked money into the till the day before? A. Yes, and spoken to Hems that day, but I found all right that day. I cannot say what other customers had paid.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Was there any gold or notes in the till? A. No; he could not have given change.
JOHN HEMS. I am a friend of Gardiner's. I received from him some marked money on the 1st of May; I gave Pitches some of it, and afterwards saw two half-crowns found on the prisoner; they were what I gave Pitches.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you know a young man who lives in the house? A. I believe there is one.
THOMAS PITCHES. I went, by desire of Hems, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning, to Gardiner's shop; I paid the prisoner four half-crowns and two shillings marked, as Hems gave me, and one shilling of my own money - I made a purchase.
BENJAMIN BEAVIS. I am a headborough. I received this money, in the prisoner's presence, from Hems; it has been in my possession ever since; the prisoner said he had put some other money into the till instead of the two half-crowns.
MR. GARDINER. It was his duty to put the money into the till immediately. I know these half-crowns by the marks.
MR. BARRY. Q. Did not you find some small silver in your till? A. Yes.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-244]
1277. JOHN THICKETT was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of April, 1 sovereign, the money of Thomas Bright.
THOMAS BRIGHT. I am a broker, and live in Queen's-place, Westminster. On the 24th of April I went to the King's Head public-house, Orchard-street, and had a pint of beer and a pipe; I gave the prisoner, who was the waiter, a sovereign - I told him to take for a pint of beer and a pipe, which he had served me with, and bring me the change; this was between eight and nine o'clock in the evening; I did not see him again till the 30th (at Queen-square;) the landlord gave me the change afterwards, on condition of my prosecuting him.
JOHN JAMES. I keep this house. The prisoner was an occasional waiter; he was there on Tuesday, the 24th of April, in that capacity. Bright frequented the house; he called for a pint of beer that day, and gave the prisoner a sovereign - he left the house immediately, without his hat on; I saw him in custody on the Saturday following. It was his duty to bring me the sovereign to change; I gave Bright the change afterwards - I asked the prisoner about it- he said he took the sovereign, and bought some clothes with it - I have not received it.
THOMAS SPEED. I am a watchman. On the 24th Jones told me of this, and on the 28th I saw the prisoner at a shop window, and took him.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-245]
1258. JAMES DONELLY, and JANE, HIS WIFE, were indicted for stealing, on the 30th of May, 1 pair of shoes, value 3s., the goods of William Fears.
MARGARET FEARS. I am the wife of William Fears, a shoemaker - we live in Tothill-street. The prisoners came in on the 30th of May - the man asked for a pair of boots to fit his wife; I tried on a pair - the man sat on a chair, and put his arm across; he took off a pair of boots, and put them into his basket, while I was trying a pair on his wife; he then objected that they were not a right and left - I showed a pair that were - they would not have them, and went away, saying they would come again in an hour; I said nothing to them, as I was alone; I followed them about four doors, then took hold of the man, and said he had a pair of shoes in his basket - he denied it; I brought them back, and the constable found them.
JAMES PROBERT. I work for William Fears. I took these boots home that evening; I hung them on a chair, and as I came out I met the prisoners going in.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOSEPH HILL. I am a constable, and found the shoes in his basket.
JAMES DONELLY's Defence. I bought them of a man on Westminster-bridge.
JAMES DONELLY - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Months.
JANE DONELLY - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-246]
1259. ANN AYRES was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of May, 1 piece of linen, containing 20 yards, value 36s., the goods of Thomas Stevens.
JOHN PIKE. I am shopman to Thomas Stevens a haberdasher, of Islington. On the 29th of May, between five and six o'clock in the evening, the prisoner came into the shop with a box, and offered ribbons and velvets for sale - we have bought of her before - I told her nothing was wanted; she had opened her box on a chair, under some Irish linen; I was serving a customer - I looked up, and saw her drawing a piece of linen off the counter - she put it into her box - I jumped over the counter, and stopped her as she went towards the door; I said, "You have got something that don't belong to you;" she did not deny it; I told her to open her box, and found it; she seemed to feel her situation, and beggen for mercy.(Property produced and sworn to.)
SAMUEL MOUNT STEVENS. I am an officer, and received her in charge.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 44.
Strongly Recommended to Mercy - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-247]
1260. JOHN ROBINS was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of May, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Plowden Presland, from his person.
PLOWDEN PRESLAND, Esq. I am a barrister. On the 26th of May, about one o'clock in the day, I was in Carey-street; I felt something at my left pocket - I put my hand down, missed my handkerchief, and saw the prisoner running from me, towards Shire-lane; I called Stop thief! and found him in custody immediately; I only lost sight of him for a moment; the officer took him across the street, and took the handkerchief from a door.
Cross-examined by Mr. CLARKSON. Q. Were there any persons about? A. Not that I saw.
JOHN CAPES. I was in Searl's-place, and heard a cry of Stop thief! I met the prisoner making towards me; I saw him throw this handkerchief into a passage; I secured him - the prosecutor came up - I gave him to an officer.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you lose sight of him? A. Not at all.
Prisoner's Defence. I was running with the mob, and never had the handkerchief.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Confined Three Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18270531-248]
1261. MARY BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of April, 5 pewter pots, value 7s., the goods of John Hatchard; and 1 pewter-pot, value 1s., the goods of James Goff.
JOHN HATCHARD. I keep the Marquis of Granby public-house, at Pimlico. I lost these pots.
THOMAS MORRIS. I am servant to Mr. Hatchard. On the 27th of April, about twenty minutes past eight o'clock, I was collecting pots - a person came to me. I went and saw the prisoner going down Raneleigh-walk, and asked if she had any pots; she said No. I turned her clothes aside, and found five.
JOHN SOUTHERWOOD. I took her in charge; she said she lived in King-street, and then that she had no lodging - I took her to the watch-house, and found Goff's pot on her.
JOHN GOFF. I keep the Crown and Anchor public-house, Ebury-street, Pimlico - this pot is mine.
GUILTY. Aged 57.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-249]
1262. GEORGE BROIDE was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of May, 1 ladder, value 5s., the goods of Edward May.
MORGAN JONES. I am a lamp-lighter. On the 23d of May, about twelve o'clock in the morning, I left this ladder in Southampton-crescent - it belongs to Edward May. The prisoner is a lamp-lighter - I never gave him leave to take it - I missed it about seven o'clock that evening - the name has been burnt out, but I know it by several marks.
THOMAS DAWE. I am a lamp-lighter, and have known the prisoner several years - I gave him 7s. for this ladder and a pair of shoes - it was put outside my door.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. It was not burnt out when I picked it up.
MORGAN JONES. When I saw him he told me he would bring it back to me.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Recommended to Mercy.
Confined Ten Days and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18270531-250]
1263. EDMUND BAILEY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of May, 1 looking-glass, value 1l., the goods of James Sansom.
SARAH WALTERS. I live with James Sansom, my father-in-law, at the Cheshire Cheese, public-house, Dean-street. On the 21st of May the prisoner had a pot of beer in the parlour; he went out with a looking-glass under his apron, which had stood over the chimney - I told Mr. Sansom, who followed and took him with it - it was not fastened to the wall.
JAMES SANSOM. I pursued the prisoner, and took him with the glass - he had come in with two men.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I went there with a friend, and was rather short of money, and went to borrow some, when I met a young man, who said "Will you earn 1s. by taking the glass for me?" - I thought it no harm.
SARAH WALTERS. There were other people in the room.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-251]
1264. JOHN VICKERY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 2 saws, value 10s., and 1 plane, value 5s., the goods of John Higginbottom; 1 plough, value 5s.; 1 saw, value 10s., and 2 planes, value 10s., the goods of John Weber Millett.
JOHN HIGGINBOTTOM. I am a carpenter, my plough and planes were in an empty house in Warwick-square, Hammersmith - I left them there about twenty minutes past twelve o'clock, and returned about one, and they were gone; I saw them again in about ten minutes - the prisoner was quite a stranger.
JOHN WEBER MILLETT. I left my tools at this house on the 13th - the prisoner was taken with them.
PETER RALPH. I am foreman at these premises - I saw the prisoner lurking about there for about twenty minutes - I afterwards met him with an apron, with these tools covered with shavings - I watched him for a mile, and every turning he came to he made a bolt to run - I at last collared him, and brought him back; the prosecutor claimed the tools.
JAMES SMITH. I am an officer, and took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 42.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-252]
1265. MARY RICHARDSON was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of April, 3 lbs. of soap, value 2s., the goods of Jane Taylor.
WILLIAM WILLMORE. I am shop-boy to Jane Taylor, who keeps an oil-shop in Ratcliff-highway. Between ten and eleven o'clock in the morning, the prisoner came in for halfpenny worth of oil; I watched, and saw her take some soap out of the window, and put it into her pocket; I took hold of her and said, "Where is that cake of soap?" she said, "What soap?" it was found in her pocket by mistress' brother, who is gone to sea.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. I am an officer, and took her - she had only a few halfpence about her.
Prisoner's Defence. He never saw me touch it.
GUILTY. Aged 68.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18270531-253]
1266. WILLIAM HOLLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of May, 1 waistcoat, value 3s., the goods of William Sharp.
WILLIAM RAY. I am an apprentice to William Sharp, a tailor and draper, of Worship-street. On the 23d of May, about ten o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner and another standing outside the shop; the prisoner went up to a suit of clothes, which hung outside the door; he unpinned a waistcoat, and put it under his coat - he crossed over to the other, and they went down an alley - I followed and saw the prisoner going in at a pawnbroker's side door - I laid hold of him with the waistcoat - the other got away.
THOMAS EAGLES. I am an officer. I received him in charge, and found the waistcoat on him, and 2s. 6d.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.

1267. ELIZABETH BROADWAY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of May, 1 blanket, value 3s.; 2 sheets, value 3s.; 1 pillow, value 2s.; 1 pillow-case, value 3d.; and 1 counterpane, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Thomas Anthony Julphs, in a lodging-room.
ELIZABETH JULPHS. I am the wife of Thomas Anthony Julphs - we live in White Lion-street, Pentonville - I let the prisoner a back-room at 3s. a week - the articles stated in the indictment were let with the room - she continued about three weeks - on Sunday afternoon I missed some articles - she came in the evening; I asked about them; she said she had pawned them, as she was in distress; she gave me the duplicate - she has three children.
WILLIAM SMITH. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Islington. I have a quilt pawned by the prisoner on the 5th of May.
WILLIAM REYNOLDS. I am an officer, and received her in charge - another pawnbroker gave up the other articles.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded poverty.
GUILTY. Aged 37.
Strongly Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18270531-255]
1268. JOHN HILLMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of May, 1 half-sovereign, 2 half-crowns, 4 shillings, and 2 sixpences, the monies of Frederick Johnson, his master.
ESTHER JOHNSON. I am the wife of Frederick Johnson,a publican - we live in Margaret-street, Hackney - the prisoner was our pot-boy. On the 9th of May, at twelve o'clock, he took out his beer, and returned for some gin - I asked him to account for his beer - he said he would rather take out the two o'clock beer first - he did so - he came back, and asked for change for a sovereign to take for the gin - I gave him half-a-sovereign, two half-crowns, and four shillings, to take to Mr. Reynolds - he did not return - he was taken on the 11th, and said he had lost the sovereign - he had taken his clothes away before that.
FREDERICK REYNOLDS. I am an attorney, and deal with Johnson. On the 9th of May I paid the prisoner 1s. 6d. for some gin - he gave me 4d. change - I did not send him for change for a sovereign.
JAMES COLEMAN. I am a watchman, and apprehended him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in liquor, and do not know whether I lost it, or spent it.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-256]
FIFTH DAY. TUESDAY, JUNE 5.
Third Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Recorder.
1269. WILLIAM HOPKINSON was indicted for embezzlement.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
JOHN RICHARDS. I am a linen-draper, and live in Oxford-street; the prisoner came to me on the 14th of August last, on trial, as an apprentice; he staid till the 29th of March, when I told him he did not suit me; he left two or three days after that; he had received monies for me, and should account for them directly he came home - Miss Cormick was a customer, and owed me 20l. 1s. 3d. - I never authorized him to promise her any deductions, provided she paid; she is a respectable lady; he never accounted to me for that bill; if he received a cheque I had always told him to bring it to me; he brought me no cheque from her, nor any money.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Where does he come from? A. Yorkshire; his family are respectable - I went down there, but did not see them; I had a good opinion of him, but he had not wit enough even to learn my private mark.
MISS ANN STAUNTON CORMICK. I live in Beaumont-street. In March last I owed Mr. Richards about 20l.; a person called and pressed me very much for it (I was not to have paid it immediately); he deducted a discount for prompt payment - I gave him a draft for 19l., on Coutts' Bank; (looking at a draft) this is it, and here is the person's receipt; 1l. 1s. 3d. was the discount - I do not recollect his person; it was a young man - I saw him write the receipt; it is signed "William Hopkinson for Mr. Richards."
JOHN RICHARDS. The receipt, and the deduction for discount, are both in the prisoner's hand-writing.
RICHARD MINSHAW. I am clerk to Mrs. Coutts - Miss Cormick cashes there - I paid this cheque, but cannot say to whom. GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-257]
1270. JOHN JAMES and DAVID WILLIAMS were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of May, 40lbs. of lead, value 4s., the goods of John Tims and Frederick Holbrooke, and fixed to a building of theirs.
AMBROSE CASTLE. I know the house of John Tims and Frederick Holbrooke, in Windmill-street, in the parish of St. Pancras; they are in possession of that and some other houses on the same estate.
THOMAS LEMON. I am watchman of Percy-street, Windmill-street, and Tottenham-court-road. On the 14th of May, about half-past two o'clock in the morning, I saw James on one side of the way, and three persons on the other side, in Tottenham-court-road - I noticed them, and saw James and Williams meet in the middle of the street, on a coach-stand, near a gas-light; they then went down on the east side, towards St. Giles' church, and at half-past three they passed between me and the lamp-post - I took particular notice of them - I saw them turn round frequently to look at me; I saw no more of them till three quarters past four o'clock, when they came down Charlotte-street; James had a load on his head, on a porter's knot; the other had nothing; they were walking together - I and Scott stood near my box; I called to James, he turned up Percy-street; I ran after him, and told Scott to take Williams, who immediately buttoned up his coat, and ran away as fast as he could - I secured James with this leaden pipe; there is above 40lbs. of it - I have compared it with No. 9. Windmill-street; it leads from the front area to the cistern; there was in the same bundle two copper coal-scuttles, and a large kettle - Scott brought Williams to the watch-house - James said he was going to some square, and that he had got the things from some back street just by; the edges of the pipe were quite bright, and I should think had been just cut.
Prisoner WILLIAMS. He said at the office, that I was one hundred yards behind James? Witness. I said the same as I do now.
JOHN SCOTT. I am a watchman. Lemon called to James to stop - Williams was close behind him, coming out of Charlotte-street; he buttoned up his coat, and ran as far as Soho-square, where a watchman turned him back, and I took him to the watch-house; he said nothing.
JOHN FREEMAN. My mother keeps the house No. 9, Windmill-street. I was there on Saturday, and all was right - I went again on Monday, and could not get in, as the chain was across the door - I saw this pipe fitted to the cistern, and have no doubt it came from there - the edges are about three parts bright, as if that was cut, and the other broken off.
JAMES' Defence. I am a porter. About a quarter-past four o'clock that morning I stood by Meux's brew-house - a man, who looked like a bricklayer, took me to Harford-street - he called me and gave me this lead to carry to Fitzroy-market.
JAMES - GUILTY. Aged 32.
WILLIAMS - GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270531-258]
1271. JOSHUA WHITE was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 3 bushels of oats, value 12s. the goods of Josiah Martin St. John; and WILLIAM GRANT and JOHN GRANT were indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
JOSIAH MARTIN ST. JOHN. I am a coach-master, and live at Tottenham. White was my hostler - Brown was my under hostler - I keep my oats in the granary, which joins the hay-loft; I had fifty or sixty quarters of oats - I missed a great quantity from time to time; I discovered a sack of oats concealed one day, and turned Brown away - White went away a few days afterwards, without any notice; I knew of no robbery till Brown afterwards came voluntarily and told me of it; William Grant is a hayjobber; John is his son; I paid a 5l. note to John Grant about a month or six weeks before I turned Brown away.
GEORGE BROWN. I was under White in Mr. St. John's service. About three months ago White proposed to me to rob master - I told him I would do nothing of the sort; he then went and got three bushels of oats from the granary, in a sack, and put into Grant's car, which stood in the yard; John Grant had brought it there, but was gone to town - William was not there - Grant is a hay-jobber; I had heard no conversation between White and him - John had brought a load of hay in the cart; he went to London to fetch another cart; he returned about half-past eleven o'clock, with a horse only, and took away the cart - I was afterwards coming back from Edmonton and met John Grant drawing the cart, with the corn in it, and a little loose hay; shortly after this White asked if I would have any thing to drink out of what Grant gave him; I said No; he said he had got some money from Mr. Grant, and if I liked to drink, or have part of it, I might; I had only seen John Grant there that morning; Mr. St. John turned me away on the 17th of May, about some corn found under the manger; I could not get a place, and came to him and gave information; nobody requested me to do so; White had left him that day; I believe it was after I gave the information; I have not spoken to White since.
Cross-examined by MR. ANDREWS. Q. Your master accused you of stealing oats? A. Yes, those under the manger; I ought to have mentioned this, but did not wish to make a disturbance; I went to tell him of it this day week, because he was blaming me to every body, and I could not get a place; I should have told him if I had got one - the Grants live at Enfield-highway; I was driving a coach when I met him in the cart, about twenty minutes past twelve o'clock; I did not speak to him; he had returned about half-past eleven; the oats were in a sack; I was going out on a coach when he came to the yard; White handed the corn from the granary; I saw him do it; I did not mean to have a shilling of the money it fetched; White told me he was going to send some hay-seeds away - I cannot swear that the sack contained corn.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Was there any hay-seed at the granary? A. No: the sack I saw in the cart was the same as I saw put into it; I had passengers on my coach when I saw him.
COURT. Q. Could you tell what was in the sack? A. No.
MR. ST. JOHN. I have missed no hay-seeds; I had not got so much as a sack; there was nothing in the granary but oats; the oats were worth 12s.
JOHN CAMP. I am constable of Edmonton. I apprehended the Grants, and told them the charge; the father said he knew nothing about it - the son said nothing; I neither threatened nor promised them any thing; as we went to the cage John said, his father, he was certain, knew nothing at all about it - but he had received them himself, and paid 5s. for them; he said so twice.
Cross-examined. Q. Did he say how he received it? A. He said it was put into his cart, as it stood in the street, and when he came back White told him he had put in some corn, and wanted 5s. for it; that he told him he had nothing but the 5l. note, which he received for the hay; that he got change and paid him 5s.
JOHN PORTER. I am hostler at the Fleece, public-house, at Edmonton. I saw John Grant taken to the cage, and heard him say "Father, you are quite innocent of this here." I did not hear him say who did receive them.
Cross-examined. Q. If he had said so, you must have heard it? A. I should think so.
JOHN GRANT's Defence. If there was any corn in the cart, it was taken out before I returned.
HENRY COTTERELL. I live in Newington-cause way, and collect the parish rents. I have known White eighteen years; I saw Brown on Sunday morning, and he denied every thing he has stated now; he said, "I will never go there at all."
JOHN CAMP. Porter was at some distance when Grant said what I have stated, and might not hear him.
WHITE - GUILTY. Aged 50.
JOHN GRANT - GUILTY.
Grant confined Six Months.
WILLIAM GRANT - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-259]
1272. JOSHUA WHITE was again indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, 3 bushels of oats, value 12s., the goods of Josiah Martin St. John; and WILLIAM SLEE was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
JOSIAH MARTIN ST. JOHN. I live at Tottenham - the prisoner was my hostler; Slee lives nearly opposite to me - he keeps pigs and a horse and cart - he sells pork, and is a general dealer - he does not deal in corn or hay; I had missed corn for four months - Brown came and gave me information; White left the same day's without notice; Slee must have known White and Brown to be my servants; Brown occasionally drove my coach.
GEORGE BROWN. I have known Slee three or four years; before Christmas last he asked me if I could get him any thing that would do for his horse; I asked if he meant hay-bands - he said No; I told him I could not - he did not say what he wanted. White said to me, one day after that, "George, if you can get any corn out, Mr. Slee will take it;" I saw White put a sack of corn on the footboard of the coach, and he said to me, "There is some corn for Slee, you can get Terry to carry it;" there was about three bushels - Terry is a labourer, but not in Mr. St. John's service; Terry came and took it away - this was about six weeks before I gave the information; I saw White, after that corn had been taken, at the Roebuck, public-house, where there is a shoe-club held; he said to me there, that he had got some money, if I liked to take part of it, I might; I owed him 1s. 3d. for my share at the club, and asked him to pay it for me, till master came home - he did so, and I paid him the next day.
Cross-examined by Mr. BARRY. Q. Did you get into trouble before you left your master? A. Not while I was with him - I was in the cage before that; I have had no quarrel with Slee - I owe him three or four shillings - he told my mother he should summons me for it; I was not in the habit of delivering parcels to him, and never saw one delivered; I had not applied to master for a character, but I intended to do so.
Mr. ADOLPHUS. Q. Did you ever know of Slee dealing with your master for corn? A. No - I was put in the cage on a charge of stealing money, but the Justice discharged me.
JAMES TERRY. I am a labourer, and live at Tottenham. George Brown sent his cousin William (a boy) to me, to say he had a parcel to take to Slee's; I went to the yard, and took it to the house - Slee was not at home - it was brought out of Mr. St. John's yard, and put on my back; White was in the yard at the time, looking in at the window, to see that all was right - Mrs. Slee was at home - I put it into the coal-hole; when I came away I saw Slee about three minutes after I left the house; I said, "I have left a parcel at your house;" he said, "It is all right;" this was on a Saturday night, between eight and nine o'clock - it was dark; I then went to the Roebuck, and had a pint of beer, and in a short time, Slee, White, and Brown, came in together; White gave me 21/2d. for my trouble; I do not know whether Slee and Brown saw that; there is a shoe-club held there.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you ever go by the name of Tush? A. Yes; people gave me that name; I have been at the stables, cleaning them, but never saw any thing wrong done; I am a bricklayer's labourer. -
JOSEPH FOSTER. I apprehended Slee, and told him he must go before a Magistrate, for receiving three bushels of oats from Mr. St. John's yard; he said, "Good God, is it possible!" I said, Yes; he then asked what he had better do; I said he must know whether he had received them or not; he said he had received no such quantity - that he had received a bushel, and about half a peck, and paid 5s. for them, which he thought the full value; he said to his wife, "I am going to Edmonton about them;" whether he said oats I cannot say.
Mr. ST. JOHN. Three bushels of oats would be worth 12s. 9d. at that time - I never sell corn at all.
JAMES TERRY. There appeared about three bushels in the sack; the coal-hole was in the shop - I left the sack there; Slee's stable is in the back yard; I do not know what was in the sack, but from the feel of it, I should think it was corn.
SLEE's Defence. These two are most vile characters, and gentlemen can prove it. I was not at home, and did not know White, nor where he lived, at all; Brown is a most base character; he told the Magistrate that White gave the oats - there was a bushel, and about half a peck; I told White, myself, I thought them very dear - but as they were left I did not send them back. I told my wife never to take in a parcel again. Brown owed me 3s. 9d. I asked him for it - he said he would send some oats, and that would make all right. Terry was in the cage last week.
JAMES TERRY. I was put into the cage for being drunk three or four times - it was always for that. I had not drank with Brown - I was never accused of any other crime. I was brought up at Tottenham, but was in the army eleven years.
GEORGE BROWN. I never offered to pay Slee with oats; he asked me to send a few oats, and that would outset us - that was since these were sent.
JAMES BROWN. I am a broker, and live on my own freehold at Tottenham. I know Brown, and would not believe him on his oath.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Have you ever heard him examined on his oath? A. No. I have known him from his childhood. I never told Mr. St. John my opinion of him.
COURT. Q. What induces you to say you would not believe him? A. He lived with a brewer, and received money; there was a warrant against him, and he absconded - he was taken and put into the cage - he was discharged; this and other circumstances make me say so.
Slee received a very good character.
WHITE - GUILTY. Aged 50.
Transported for Seven Years.
SLEE - GUILTY. Aged 30.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-260]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1233. CHARLES CHAMBERS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of April, 6 guns, value 40s.; 1 gun-barrel, value 4s., and 120 ram-rods, value 30s., the goods of John George Lacey and David William Wilson.
MESSRS. BRODRICK and BARRY conducted the prosecution.
JOHN GEORGE LACEY. I am in partnership with David William Wilson - we are gun-makers, and live in Fenchurch-street - Chambers worked some years for me - he lived in Lambeth-street - he used to take gun-stocks home, and return them finished; our method of doing business is for me to deliver a note to the prisoner, which he takes to Ward, who delivers the stocks to him - we have two or three thousand on our premises; the prisoner used to take away a number of stocks and barrels, and the small furniture for them. In consequence of information I went to Mrs. Wood, in an alley, in Bunhill-row, with Plaistow, early in March - I there found six muskets with our mark on them, a quantity of ramrods, a Dutch barrel, and a barrel guage. Morecock had given me information that morning.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. How do you know the muskets? A. They have the mark L. and W., which we always put - we never sold them, for they are not quite finished; they are guns, but not finished - they have no triggers, but they are guns - we had no other outdoor workman - we had six or seven workmen who had goods of this description - there is no mark on the stock, but on the barrel - we never sell them in that state - guns of manufacturers are sometimes mixed, but no man takes in any but his own - this stock has had a mark on it, but it has been sawed off.
JOHN WARD. I am in the prosecutor's employ. It was my duty to give out stock-guns. I delivered the prisoner out some goods the latter end of the year - we had great confidence in him - we allow those men, whom we have confidence in, to come into the warehouse, but no others - we gave guns out in twenty-five or fifty in a lot, and very seldom give out thirty; such a thing might happen - I bring out five at a time from the warehouse - the prisoner has, perhaps, taken them from my arms, or I might putthem just at the door, or he might come into the warehouse - he would take the furniture with them, according to what number he had; the small work is kept up-stairs - he used to take the work away in a truck, which he and a boy brought.
Cross-examined. Q. You deliver them to a boy? A. He sometimes brought a boy with him.
COURT. Q. Did he ever send a boy alone? A. He has I believe sent a boy when he has been busy - there was one other man whom we placed confidence in - he would have them delivered out in the same way; the prisoner could not get into the stock-room when I was not there - I kept the key, but I might be at another part of the premises - I do not think he could take twenty-five guns, and the materials without my knowledge - I delivered him the last lot about November - I enter them on a slate, and a clerk enter them in a book - he is not here, but the books are - I know he had some work home in February - all the work, which goes out and comes in, are entered; and these entries have always tallied - I cannot say how long I have left him in the warehouse; he might have taken guns without my knowing it - nobody else is in the warehouse.
MR. BRODRICK. Q. Was he employed by your master the end of last year? A. Yes; I cannot tell how many indoor workmen we had, but we had no out-door workman then.
COURT. Q. He could not take thirty guns without your knowing it? A. No - he sometimes took his own work himself.
WILLIAM CHARLES HAINES. I was a gun-screwer in the prosecutors' employ, and left him on the 4th of November. I remember seeing a Dutch gun on his premises. I have since that seen the barrel at Plaistow's. There was a guage there.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How do you know it? A. The breech nail-hole is not exactly in the middle - that might happen to others.
COURT. Q. How long had you seen it in his premises? A. Ever since I have been there, which is about twelve months, and I left it there - the prosecutors' servants used to come there at times. I do not know whether they could see it - it might be behind other guns.
WILLIAM MORECOCK. I was in the prosecutors' employ, from October last, till May. I was a screwer - the prisoner gave me one hundred and twenty-one rods, and one black Dutch barrel, and a guage in April - he told me to take them to my own house; I did so, and returned them to him by his order - he came in just as I was going away, and asked if I had any friend, who I could leave them with for a week - I said in two or three minutes that I thought I did know a person; Mrs. Ann Wood, of No. 42, Banks'-court; Blue Anchor-alley, Bunhill-row; he told me to leave them for a week. I took them there; he said he could go for them, and if he said his name was Chambers, she was to let him have them. I told Wood so - I continued to work for him until the 5th of May, I told him what I had done - after this I heard of the prosecutors' being robbed, and gave them information. Mr. Lacy went with me, Ward and the officer, to Wood's. I found the goods in the same state as I left them; we took them out of the cellar there and gave them to the officer.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. How long have you known these goods at the prisoners? A. From November - they were not there before; not on the part of the premises where I was. Lacy's servants called occasionally about work - the Dutch barrel was in the cupboard, which was not locked - there were six English guns and one India gun, in the same closet, the room was locked when we were absent. Only two lads worked there besides me - I heard of the robbery about the 2d of May, from a stranger whom I met in Lambeth-street. I did not see the prisoner after that - I worked for him that evening. I thought he had a reason for sending them away - I have been at Birmingham to work since; I gave the prisoner no notice of going - I lived there from June to August last year - I took nothing with me.
Q. Now; how much did you pay him when you returned? A. Twelve shillings for the rent of my room - he did not charge me with any offence. I went to work with him immediately - I had some unfinished work of his before I went.
MR. BRODRICK. Q. You worked at piece-work and thought proper to go to Birmingham? A. Yes; I left the prisoners working in town - I took my wife with me, being ill. When I was at the prisoners we worked in the second floor back room, the cupboard was in that room. I returned to work with the prisoner as soon as he had work: which was in October.
COURT. Q. Did you tell the prisoner you had left the work unfinished? A. No; he employed me again - the work was always in the open shop.
HENRY FINCHEN. I was in the prosecutor's employ the latter part of last year - I saw a Dutch barrel there, and have seen the same since at Plaistow's - I brought thirty guns to the prisoner's in a truck the beginning of November - he brought them down the prosecutor's passage himself, and gave them to me - I took them away in the truck - I left them in Fenchurch-street, and Morecock took them the rest of the way. I counted out the furniture for them when they got home, there were twenty-five sets of furniture, and thirty guns. I took fifty guns there by the prisoner's direction the latter end of November.
ANN WOOD. I am a widow, and live in Bank's-court, Blue Anchor-alley, Bunhill-row - Morecock called on me on the 25th of April with some guns, I did not count them - they were put into my cellar - he said, "I wish you would let me leave these guns for a week, for Mr. Chambers, my master;" and if Chambers came I was to let him have them - I saw some rods in the cellar; the officer came and took them away.
WILLIAM PLAISTOW. I am an officer I went to Wood's, and brought away the property - I apprehended the prisoner - the gun-locks were only forced into the stock, and in the coach some of them fell out.
Mr. LACEY. I went to Wood's, and brought these things away - I identify all the barrels, and some of the stocks; but they have not all got my mark on them.
Prisoner's Defence. I am innocent.
GEORGE WILBRAHAM. I am a gun-maker, and live in Leadenhall-street. I have known the prisoner twenty-five years; the prosecutor's guns have been sent to me instead of my own; but I always sent them back.
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor and Jury.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18270531-261]
1274. MARY CALLOW was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of January, 10lbs. weight of beef, value 5s.; 10lbs. weight of mutton, value 5s.; 10lbs. weight of bacon, value 5s.; 10lbs. weight of ham, value 5s.; 10lbs. weight of butter, value 5s.; 20lbs. weight of bread, value 5s.; 10 sovereigns, 10 half-sovereigns, 10 crowns, 10 half-crowns, ten shillings, and 10 sixpences, five 10l., ten 5l., ten 2l., and ten 1l. Bank notes, the property of George Hume, her master, in his dwelling-house.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
GEORGE HUME. I am a solicitor, and live at No. 8, Great James-street, Bedford-row. I am married, and have a family - the prisoner was in my service from July to January. I and my family left town in October, and returned in November; the prisoner and Kelly (another servant) were left at home. On the 23d of December we went to Richmond, leaving nobody in the house but the prisoner, Kelly, and Mr. Smith, my brother-in-law; we returned on the 8th of January, and the prisoner said Kelly had been in the habit of having men living and sleeping with her, and unless she was dismissed she would not stay; Kelly was then dismissed; and two days after we sent the prisoner away, not respecting dishonesty but her morals, for doing precisely the same thing. I paid her 5s. I think - I believe there was about 1l. due to her - I have not paid her the rest - it has been demanded - she applied for a character on the 24th of January - my wife gave her a written one - she and her mother came to the house next day, and after that the prisoner summoned me for the 15s.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. What complaint did Kelly make of the prisoner? A. She said she had taken bread and meat and butter - the prisoner said Kelly had done the same - my wife was confined to her room, or she generally settles wages.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. What did Kelly say? A. She said victuals had been sent out of the house once or twice a-week.
COURT. Q. Who gave instructions for this indictment? A. I did. I told the Grand Jury I did not accuse her of stealing the money.
ELIZABETH KELLY. I lived as servant to Mr. Hume. The prisoner was there before me - she was cook; while master was away the last time there was victuals sent out; one day I remember a loaf of home-made bread, between a quartern and a half quartern, and about a pound of round of beef - she went out with it, and returned without it; she said it was for her brother John; I told master of it after I left.
Cross-examined. Q. The prisoner had complained of you? A. Yes; I had my mother there once or twice, and a man once or twice a week - they had victuals there. I said nothing about any money - Mr. Hume sent to me - I went and told him.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. By whose advice did you admit this man? A. By Callow's; the man who came to see her, brought the one who came to me. I was summoned in January; I told every thing to Mr. Hume then.
COURT. Q. When you told this did you know you had lost your place through her? A. Yes.
WILLIAM SAUNDERS. I am a constable of Richmond. I took the prisoner into custody for stealing bread; she said she took her brother a loaf now and then.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270531-262]
1175. JOHN DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of May, 5 lamps, value 12s., the goods of John Frederick James.
JEREMIAH MOGG. I am a waiter at the Bedford Arms tea-gardens, Camden-town. These lamps were all safe at two o'clock on Friday, the 19th of May - they were not used that night. Next morning, about nine o'clock, I missed five, and saw oil spilled near the bottle-rack, where they are kept - I found the prisoner in custody with them. John Frederick James is owner of the gardens. They were locked at night, and a watchman placed at each gate.
Prisoner. Q. Was there other lamps more valuable there? A. Yes.
DANIEL PARNELL. I am a watchman. On Saturday morning, the 19th of May, about nine o'clock, I saw the prisoner about half a mile from the gardens; I followed him, and called to Russell, who stopped him; I took these lamps from him, tied up in a cloth; he was in Jeffries-street, Kentish-town; he turned from me, and went away.
STEPHEN RUSSELL. I am a watchman. I stopped the prisoner with the bundle, and made him untie it; I found these five lamps in it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
ABRAHAM LONG. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house. I found a double key, and some knives and forks on him.
Prisoner's Defence. He says the gate was locked; I could not get in; I picked them up in the bundle.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.

THOMAS JONES. I saw the sovereign given to the prisoner; he was to pay a coach, and bring the change to the prosecutrix; I do not know whether he paid the coach, which stood at the door.
MRS. FARNER. The coachman never called again for the money.
NOT GUILTY.

1277. MARY ANN TICHBOURNE was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of May, 7 shillings, the monies of William Catchpole.
WILLIAM CATCHPOLE. On the 19th of May I was in Osnaburg-street, Regent's-park, and met the prisoner; I had 7s. in my waistcoat pocket; we were talking together, and I found her hand in my pocket, and told her she had taken my money - she denied it; I took her to the watch-house, and gave her in charge. I am certain it was safe just before; she said all she had about her was sixpence and some halfpence. The watchman let her go; I stopped heragain, and took her to another watchman, who made her take the seven shillings out of her mouth.
THOMAS SWILES. I am a watchman, and took her - she gave me the seven shillings from her mouth.
Prisoner's Defence. He gave them to me.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.

1278. BENJAMIN RAY was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of May, 3 pewter pots, value 2s. 8d., the goods of Thomas Meecham.
THOMAS MEECHAM. I keep the Duke of Cumberland public-house, Red Lion-street, Spitalfields; the prisoner was brought in on the 10th of May, with a pint pot under his coat; I fetched a constable, who found two quart pots on him.
JOHN FITZGERALD. On the 10th of May I was at my house, No. 3, Red Lion-street; I saw the prisoner go up the steps of No. 2, and take some pots from the window cill; he had sweep's clothes on; I secured him, and took him to Meecham's; he said he was going to take them to the public-house, but he was going the wrong way - he dropped one.
THOMAS HART. I am a constable, and took him in charge.
THOMAS GOODLAD. I am a baker. I saw the prisoner and witness going along; I saw the prisoner take one pot from under his clothes, and put it on a potatoe sack; I took it to the watch-house.
ELIZABETH PRATT. I live in Red Lion-street. On the 10th of May I put some pots out of the window.
GUILTY. Aged 17.

1279. THOMAS LAWSON was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of April, 3 pewter pots, value 3s., the goods of Richard Taylor; 1 pewter pot, value 1s., the goods of Edward Smith; and 2 pewter pots, value 2s., the goods of John Alford.
RICHARD TAYLOR. I keep the Earl St. Vincent, public-house, Torrington-street. On the 9th of April Green brought three pots of mine in. I know nothing of the prisoner.
JAMES RUSSELL. I am servant to John Alford of the Calthorpe Arms, public-house, Gray's-inn-road - these pots were put out at our door. - I missed two about half-past ten o'clock. Green afterwards brought them to the house.
JOHN GREEN. I am a beadle. I stopped the prisoner in Russell-square - I had seen him loitering about; he went into Torrington-street three times - I went up and found these pots on him.
EDWARD SMITH. I keep a public-house in Torrington-street. This is my pot.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had found the pots in Bloomsbury-square, and representing himself to be in great distress.
GUILTY. Aged 46.

1280. SARAH JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of May, 12 books, value 4l., the goods of Lewis Duval, Esq.
WILLIAM ANGERS. I am shopman to a pawnbroker in Liquorpond-street. On the 17th of May the prisoner pawned this book. On the 25th she brought this other, which is Rowe's Cases of Bankruptcy - when she was gone I saw the erasure of a name in it - and when she came again I stopped her.
JAMES KING. I am a pawnbroker. I have some books which were pawned with me in March last, at different times; I believe by the prisoner - but I cannot swear to her.
WILLIAM CREE. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Gray's-inn-lane; I have some books, pawned by the prisoner.
ELIZABETH PARK. I am laundress at Mr. Duval's chambers, Lincoln's-inn. I employed the prisoner there under me - I gave her a few half-pence now and then.
JAMES EDWARD WATERHOUSE. I am clerk to Mr. Lewis Duval, in Lincoln's-inn - those books are his, and were in his chambers.
WILLIAM LEE. I am an officer. I took up the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in great distress, and this lady employed me.
GUILTY. Aged 26.

1281. HENRY HOLMES was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of April, 2 live tame pigeons, price 3s. 6d., the goods of Roger McDonald.
ROGER M'DONALD. I keep the Harrow, public-house, at Poplar . On the 16th of April I went into the tap-room. Fordham pointed out Holmes to me - he then opened his coat and the pigeons dropped down - they were taken from the house in my yard.
Cross-examined by MR. QUIN. Q. Was any body else in the tap-room? A. Yes; I swear they are mine.
WILLIAM FORDHAM. On the 16th of April, in the evening, I was going in at McDonald's back gate - and saw the prisoner on a ladder, reaching the pigeons out of their house. I went down to the stable, and when I returned he was on the ground - I asked what he did there, he then went into the tap-room - I went and told McDonald - it was dark. I was going to feed the horse.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he sober? A. Yes.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Nine Days.

1285. JOHN WILLIAMSON was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of May, 56lbs. of lead, value 5s., the goods of Joseph French, and fixed to a certain dwelling-house of his.
JAMES FOGG. I am an officer of the Thames Police. On the evening of the 30th May I found the prisoner in Hooper-square, among some carpenters, and builders, a basket laid there; they pointed out the prisoner, and said he had got the lead - I collared him, and asked where the lead was; he said in the basket, and that he had found it in a field - I asked him again, where it came from; he said, "Well, it came from Limehouse, near the Royal Duke public-house; I cut it from a shop-front - I had been out of work a long time" - I took it to French's, where he described, and compared it - it fitted exactly, and appeared fresh cut.
JOSEPH FRENCH. I keep a chandler's-shop at Lime-house; this lead matched exactly with my shop.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-273]
1286. JOHN CHARLES PRINCE was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of May, 1 quart of wine, value 2s., and 1 bottle, value 2d, the goods of William Pudney.
WILLIAM PUDNEY. I keep the Three Johns public-house, at Pentonville . On the 29th of May I left the key in the wine cellar-door; I went down, and missed one bottle from a bin which I had seen safe that morning, it had laid very smooth, and if a bottle was gone I should know it; the wine had been there for six months; the prisoner was my pot-boy, and had access to that cellar; on the day following I left the key in the door on purpose, when he was going down to fetch a pail of water - I afterwards went into my bed-room, and saw him, (while he was washing his pots,) pour some wine out of a bottle, into a pint pot, and drink it - I had before this seen the bottle in the wash-house - I went down, and the bottle was gone - I told my wife; she said, "I will call him to tea;" I then went down again, and found the same bottle filled with gin - I fetched a constable; and he denied the whole of it- I knew the bottle by these two distinct marks of mud on it, which it got in the cellar.
Cross-examined by MR. QUIN. Q. But if your bottles had been elewhere, might they not have got some dirt on them? A. Yes - I missed the bottle on the evening of the day he drank the wine; I told him I was going out for an hour, and then I went to my bed-room; I saw it empty after he drank the wine, and I then missed it for about three minutes.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you mean, that while you went to speak to your wife about the constable, it was filled with gin? A. Yes.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been there but two days; he said he had had a young man who had robbed him, and he should be very strict upon me - I said he might; this bottle of gin had been in the cellar nine months or more, and having a few words with my master, he got it up and accused me of it.
WILLIAM PUDNEY. He had a few words the night before, with my wife, because he got tipsy, and could not settle his beer.
JURY. Q. Did the pint pot hold all the wine? A. There was one-half the wine gone out of the bottle at twelve o'clock, and the remainder he drank out of the pot - I had a good character with him.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270531-274]
1287. GEORGE HARRISON was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of April, 1 ring, value 1s. 6d.; 1 shirt, value 3s.; 1 waistcoat, value 1s., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 2s., the goods of George Harrison.
GEORGE HARRISON. I am the prisoner's father; he lived with me. I have taken him several times from prison before - these articles were in a chest of drawers in my room; they were not locked. I went to bed with him on the 1st of April - he got up early the next morning, left me in bed, and took these things with him - I had seen them all safe in the afternoon.
Prisoner. Q. Is there any marks on them? A. There was on the shirt; I know the ring by its general appearance.
WILLIAM JONES. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned a ring with me, in the name of Edward Palmer - I am certain of his person.
Prisoner. Q. How do you know me? A. I remember you very well - I made out the duplicate - I believe him to be the man.
CHARRES JACOBS. I saw the prisoner in Oxford-street one day, about the middle of April - he said, "Charles, I have the ticket of a nice shirt and handkerchief to sell;" he asked 1s. for them, and said he wanted the moneythen; I gave him 1s., went to the pawnbroker's, and go them out. Here they are.
WILLIAM DAVIS. I am a watchman. On the 24th of April I took the prisoner in Adam and Eve-court. I found nothing on him.
HENRY RICHARD BUCKERIDGE. Davis gave me the duplicate of a ring.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. It is false about my father's taking me out of prison. As he has come here to expose me, I think it my duty to expose him - he has been convicted here, and sentenced for fourteen years himself.
GEORGE HARRISON. I was tried here for buying property, which I did not know was stolen, and received sentence of fourteen years' transportation.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
